## 0. What the old app already knows (written before any brief; every number is the old app's)

Read in full by this session itself: TRIED-BEFORE 6, 17, 20, 21, 27; COPY-CHECKS-STUDY (all); LESSONS 3, 4;
FEATURES 12 rounds 13 and 14; answers 4, 5, 8; the old app's `ix_held.go`, `ix_copies.go`, `ix_subst.go`
(`wt-v12.31`); the new app's `internal/lookalike`, `internal/catalog/ladder.go`, `internal/exact`, and how the
engine and `internal/supply` finish a check.

**The look-alike rule (`ixCheapSame`, `ixCoversSaved`, `ixSavedCopyOf`, `ixHeldTriage`):**
- Videos only, from 256 KiB (5 MB until 12.16), not self-destructing. Both sides need a length above 0, pixel
  size, and a preview hash. Alike: lengths within 500 ms, aspect within 2% of the saved file's, preview hashes
  at most 6 bits apart. The saved copy must cover it: no fewer pixels, at least 90% of the bytes.
- Costs no request. Every queued video was looked at every round (2 minutes), with no cap since 12.16.
- Why the new app's present rule ("same pixels, size within 1%") does not do: it misses re-encoded copies, and
  those were the measured waste. Byte-identical waste was small (93 files, 0.4 GB in a day).
- The caps that cost: a list cut at the 20,000 largest let about 450 copies (4.35 GB) a day through under
  16 MB; the 5 MB floor another 619 a day (1.4 GB). So: no cap on what is compared, floor 256 KiB.
- The 6 bits: on saved twins checked by fingerprints, right 95% at 6 bits or fewer, 76% at 7 to 12, 3% at
  13 to 20. Only even distances occur (0, 2, 4, 6).
- How right a hold is: about 5% of holds by the preview rule were wrong among small files (PLAN-small-dedup).
  Of the holds over 200 MiB that a frame check judged: against a saved file 1,218 confirmed and 275 released;
  against a queued file 130 confirmed and 400 released.
- No stored fact separates a wrong hold from a right one (preview distance, length, pixels, size: the
  released ones are, if anything, closer). A tighter threshold would lose right holds and keep the wrong ones.
  **So the thresholds stay as they are.**
- A hold against a queued file was right in 130 of 530 judged. **So it is counted as "would hold" before it
  holds.**
- Held now in the old app (11-13): 39,798 files held against a saved file (1,990 GB), 1,544 against a queued
  one (150 GB).
- The preview hash of `internal/lookalike` equals the old app's on four invented previews (answers 4, E.13).

**What the old app did that the ladder cannot do at cataloguing time:** a video queued before its copy was
saved was still caught, because every queued video was compared again each round. The new app judges a file
once, when its message is catalogued. A new account reads history first and downloads after, so most pairs
are both queued when judged. This needs a step when a file is saved (the wanted videos it covers are held
then): not in piece (a); listed under "next".

**The exact check (`ix_subst.go`; ten days):** 9,046 checks, 5,007 files (240 GB) not downloaded, all by
Telegram's hashes; 18 checks without hashes; no match ever undone, none ever checked again. Queued files of
256 KiB or more with a saved file of exactly their size: 30,714 of 50,245; 12% of those have more than one.
The new app's first real run: 15 checks, 43 hash requests, no wait; one answer carries up to 8 windows of
128 KiB.

**Why a held file of the same byte size goes to the exact check first:** of the 275 look-alike holds a frame
check released, 203 had the saved file's exact byte size, pixels and length, and at least 23 were exact
copies (18 skipped later by Telegram's hashes, 5 the same SHA-256 after download). Of the 1,218 confirmed,
975 had the same byte size. So most look-alike holds are candidates for an exact answer, which ends them
without bytes and without a frame check.

**The frame check (for piece c, not started):** its "copy" is right (184 of 188 judged); its "no match"
against a named file was wrong about 3 times in 4 (882 of 1,140 paired after download). 8 parts a check at
the median; 10 to 22 s per check in unlimited hours, 3 to 14 minutes under the speed limit where about half
time out. Kept parts: at least 63 GB reused in 7,916 downloads, none refused. 99.6% of saved videos are MP4
or MOV. The trouble of 12.13 to 12.27 came from holding a download slot while waiting on Telegram.

**The owner's decisions that bind this:** only exact evidence ends a file without bytes; a probable copy is
held with its evidence; a frame check's "no match" never releases a file alone; on a live account a held copy
waits for good unless the owner releases it, with the file it matches shown (11-14 22:46).

## 2. Piece (b): same byte size goes to the exact check first (not started)

How it can be built without touching the engine (this session's reading of `engine/check.go` and
`supply/pending.go`, to be checked again when briefed):
- The engine already hands a checked file back held when the Checker answers "probable"
  (`CheckSupply.CheckHeld`, `catalog.HoldExact`), with no attempt spent.
- So: a video the ladder finds alike to a saved file, with a saved file of exactly its byte size, is not
  held at once: it stays wanted with a mark, at its own rank. At its turn the exact check runs. "Same": linked,
  no bytes. Anything else ("different", "unknown", no candidate left, the setting off, the wait rule): the
  Checker answers "probable" for a marked file, and it is held as a look-alike with its evidence, and with
  what the hashes said.
- **Known gap, to decide when briefed:** three outcomes are made by the engine itself and end in the download
  whatever the Checker says: a wait Telegram stated on the hash call, the check's deadline, a panic. For a
  marked file that would download a probable copy. The old app's number for how often: 18 checks without an
  answer in 9,046. Closing it needs a few lines in `internal/engine` (the integrator's), or it is accepted
  and counted.

- 11-15 09:22: THE OWNER asked in this session: "If the file is on the cusp, can it automatically adjust the number of samples it takes/fingerprints? Or have a setting for it?" Taken as a standing request (board message 6 asks the integrator to write it into FEATURES 12). Design for brief E6, to build on `wip/copies-4` once E5 is back:
  - What the old app did: always 8 points, visited 0,7,3,5,1,6,2,4, stop early when decided; a copy needs max(3, 60% of the frames read); 3 or more hits short of that is "partly the same"; fewer is "no match". Nothing was ever sampled twice.
  - Its numbers: "copy" right in 184 of 188 judged; "no match" against a named file wrong about 3 times in 4 (882 of 1,140 paired after download; 477 as the same video); of look-alike holds it released, at least 23 of 275 were exact copies. A check cost 8 parts at the median, and the parts are reused by the download (63 GB reused, none refused), so more frames of a file that is then downloaded cost almost nothing.
  - "On the cusp" (proposed, to be counted from the first real checks): after the first round, (1) some frames match one candidate but fewer than a copy needs (1 hit up to the need minus 1: this includes "partly the same"); (2) no frame matches, but the file is a look-alike of that candidate by the preview rule, or has its byte size, pixels and length; (3) fewer than 3 frames could be read (flat or unreadable frames) and more points exist. Not on the cusp: a copy already; or no hit and no other evidence.
  - Second round: 8 more points between the first (the midpoints), same visiting idea, the same early stop, the verdict over all frames read with the same rule (60%, at least 3) and the same 32-part cap for both rounds together. Never more than two rounds unless the setting says so.
  - Setting: automatic (default), always the larger sample, off (the old app's behaviour). Counted per hour: second rounds run, and how each changed the answer (to copy, to partly, unchanged), so the first real days say whether it pays.
  - Unchanged rules: a "no match" still never releases a file alone; a frame check never gives "exact".
  - Where: the pure part (`SampleTimes` for a round, the cusp test) in `internal/footage`; the points to read as an argument of the ffmpeg part's `Sample`.

Written down for them: `archiver/docs/progress/copies-vfp-COLLECTOR.md` on `wip/copies-11` (start the worker, `NotYet`, `Wake`, `Staged` with the save's "saved before" flag: section 6 says what `supply` and the writer's hook lack); for the engine: the part size must be 1 MiB, the catalogue is closed no sooner than 10 s after `Engine.Wait`, `Put`'s errors are never printed (entry C8).

**The next pieces, in order (the design is `DESIGN-COPIES-frame-check.md`; nothing of them is briefed):**
1. **E15, the catalogue and the supply's side of the frame check.** Schema step 19 (taken on the board for this; check the number is still free of others' use, the mirror's M5 took 20): the kept parts' table (**it must reference `tg_media(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`**: parts are keyed by the catalogue's number, entry C8), the probe's record, an index of saved videos with a fingerprint by length (`VideoCandidates` finds only videos with a preview hash: E10's review B12). The catalogue's ledger for `internal/parts` as an adapter package (as `internal/storeledger` is for the store), with `needs` for the sweep. The supply: the decision at `Next` (a video of 20 MB or more with a length, not resumed, with at least one candidate: one index read; a file with no candidate is handed out exactly as today), `Probed` (the file aside "being judged" until T by the door `CheckLater` uses: no new `c_want` state), **no plan again in the run after a `Requeue` with a probe's reason (`engine.IsProbeRefusal`)**, the engine's `ProbeCounters` into the hourly table. Read first: `supply.Next` (how a marked file is passed over, not cut), `pending.go`, `catalog/exactcheck.go` `CheckLater`, `supply/hourly.go`. Cut it from a head that has C7, C8 and C9 (it builds on all three).
2. **E16, the judge** (a new package: the plan from `mp4index.PartsFor` over kept parts, `footage/ffmpeg.Check` with the candidates, the verdict written in one transaction; "copy": held with its evidence and its kept parts dropped; "partly" and "no match": wanted, parts kept; more parts needed: at most 3 rounds and 32 parts; unknown: 15 minutes or an hour, after 24 hours downloaded unchecked, counted). Never a verdict from a limit hit.
3. **The collector's lines** for the worker, the part store and the judge (after G1).
4. **Confirm or release** (board step 2d): a held look-alike probed: "copy" confirms, anything else leaves it held; the owner's two decisions above (the reader of the viewer's `decisions.sqlite`: board message 13 and my answer under it, three things asked of the viewer session, no answer seen yet; the re-judging when a saved file is lost: the edge outlives the file today, E12's review N1); lesser copies behind the best.
5. **Listed and unbuilt in this track's packages:** a pass over library videos that have no fingerprint (read only while no copy waits; it also settles the permanent "timeout" rows); ffmpeg looked for in the program's own folder first (the integrator's message 40) in `internal/footage/ffmpeg`.

**Lessons of this session, for whoever continues:**
- **Merge the main head into a branch BEFORE its full runs.** E12's named tests passed on the branch and two of them failed on the merged tree (another track's statement had changed under them); the first whole run was wasted on a head that was not the last.
- **Read the other tracks' board messages before telling them what your piece gives them.** I told Core its settle got the look-alike judgement "for nothing"; its scenario test showed the gap (a file with no edge), and the fix was in another place (the step at save).
- **A brief's guard can be wrong:** my "never mark a file found different" was file-wide where the record is per pair; the reviewer found it. Ask the coder to say where a rule is read from, and have the reviewer check the brief's rules too, not only the code.
- **Every piece had real defects after all named tests and mutations passed** (a pinned core, parts thrown away at a stop, a burst drained 20 times slower, a put through a planted link). One independent review, its fixes, and a short second pass of the same reviewer on the fix round each time: the second passes found new defects three times.
- **A coder resumed in the same session keeps its context:** small follow-ups (5 to 20 minutes) went to the same coder by a message. That ends with the session.
- **Write times from `date`, not from memory** (two entries above were minutes ahead and were corrected).
- Runs: `ix-tools/full-runs.sh <copy> <out> "<packages>" "<race packages>"`; a package with a slash in its path (`footage/ffmpeg`) is run by hand beside it; `FFMPEG_PATH` must be exported for the ffmpeg and vfp tests (the second entry of `where.exe ffmpeg` is the 9.0.2 build); two race containers at once need about 8 GB: run them one after the other when less is free.

- **Decided in the brief, with the reason (where it adds to `DESIGN-COPIES-frame-check.md`):**
  - **The supply plans without reading a part:** the first plan is the first and the last part (where an MP4's index lives; both reused by the transfer); every later plan is the judge's, stored in `c_probe.plan`. So `Next` does no disk I/O and needs no index reader.
  - **A file being judged is set aside until a time** by `CheckLater`'s own statement shape (no new `c_want` state, no attempt spent), reason "its frame check is being judged"; a judge that never answers leaves nothing stuck: the file comes back and the supply settles it as an unknown (`ProbeOverdue`, why `no-answer`).
  - **The unknown's bounds are the old app's, number for number:** put off 15 minutes when the round brought new parts, 1 hour otherwise; downloaded unchecked only after 24 hours of unknowns (a gap of 6 hours starts the count again) or 3 identical ffmpeg failures with no new part, or at once for a failure in the file itself (no index, cannot seek, bad data, too few frames, over 32 parts or 3 rounds); each counted with its why in `c_probe`.
  - **One place holds the rules:** `ProbeVerdict` (the judge's door, built now so that E16 is a pure package); its kind has no value for "exact"; a held file stays held after every kind ("no match" never releases).
  - **Candidates by the fingerprint's own length** (the old app's `ix_vfp.duration`; closes E10's review B12), only files whose bytes the archive has (a lost file is never a candidate).
  - **Behind `supply.Config.FrameCheck`** (the judge's "I run and ffmpeg passed"): nil or false, `Next` is bit for bit today's. A plan the lane refuses is never planned again in the run and is on record; after "no part store" the supply plans nothing more.

- **What the catalogue needs (a schema step, the next free number then):** a table of the owner's pairs as the catalogue's own rows (both media, the verdict, when, when applied), so that (1) rung 6 (`ladder.go` `lookAlikeVideo`, which today takes the first passing saved video, one of the same byte size first) skips a pair the owner called different and goes on to the next saved video; (2) the frame check's candidates leave that saved file out for that queued file and a "copy" against it is never written; (3) "same" marks the hold confirmed by the owner and changes nothing else (a click is not bytes: the file stays held, never skipped).
- **"Not the same":** the probable edge of that pair goes; the file is judged again by the ladder's own rule without that pair: held against another saved file if one still covers it, else wanted in its turn (with the exact check first when a saved file has its byte size, C7's mark). A verdict taken back removes the catalogue's row; the file is judged again then too.
- **A held copy whose saved look-alike is lost (item 27):** `staging.go` `WantAgain` is where a file becomes lost (one transaction, per media of the file). Today the probable edge of OTHER media that point at the lost file's media outlives it (E12's review N1), so their hold stays for good. To build: in that transaction, every held media with a probable edge to a media of the lost file is judged again (rung 6 again without the lost file: `lookAlikeVideo` already skips a lost file): wanted unless another saved file covers it. `staging.go` is Core's file: ask on the board before the brief names it, or build it as a door of mine that `WantAgain` calls in one line.

**11-16 00:11 (C3): THE OWNER, to this session (00:09): "The stuck verdict seems like a weak spot. Can there be parallel checks, or perhaps a stricter timeline? Consider it please." Taken: both, plus a cap on checks in flight; written into both fix briefs and sent to both fix coders (00:11).**
- **What the weak spot was, wider than the one finding:** one judge worker, so one slow or stuck file delays every file behind it; and a file whose own check keeps failing stayed in limbo up to 24 hours. The 24 hours were the old app's bound UNDER THE SPEED LIMIT (a check with no answer might get one hours later); **with the limit off the old app gave a failed check one more try and then downloaded (`ix_dl_check.go` line 361), and a video that waited over 2 minutes for a check slot downloaded.** Here no check waits on Telegram and (after finding 2 of E15's review) a retry costs no request.
- **E16b, added (`BRIEF-E16b-fixes.md`, "Added 11-16 00:11"):** P1 checks in parallel, `Config.Workers`, default 2 (the study's E.7 number; a check starts at most two one-thread ffmpeg processes: at most 4 of 12 cores at peak), 1 to 4; a file never in two workers' hands; shared state under one lock; the self-test once. P2 a verdict the door does not take: 3 tries by its worker (1, 2, 4 s), then aside, tried once a minute, **dropped after 5 minutes** (not 10), no worker waits on it. P3 counts so that the first run says whether 2 is right (most checks at once, most files waiting, seconds a check).
- **E15b, added (`BRIEF-E15b-fixes.md`, "Added 11-16 00:11"):** T1 two kinds of unknown: the file's own (`timeout`, `ffmpeg`, `ffmpeg-io`, `parts-missing`, `no-answer`): put off 5 minutes, **3 in a row with no new part and the file is downloaded unchecked, counted** (about 15 minutes for a check that times out, about 30 for a stuck verdict, instead of up to 24 hours); the system's (`no-ffmpeg`, `not-kept`, `flood`): unchanged, 24 hours, nothing let through meanwhile. T2 a file waits for its judge 5 minutes, not 15. T3 **at most 16 checks in flight** (the old app: 10 at once): while 16 files have an open check the supply makes no first plan (the video stays wanted, other files go on), so a lane full of eligible videos cannot fill the judge's list faster than it is judged and push innocent files past their 5 minutes.
- **Asked of the owner in my reply (one question, my recommendation: keep it):** whether the 24 hours should also be shortened for a system-wide failure (ffmpeg missing or failing its self-test, no room for kept parts). Kept at 24 hours until the owner says otherwise: letting every candidate video through after a short wait is the costly error; the state is visible in the judge's hourly lines.

The second passes of both reviewers cover the additions; the race detector on `internal/probejudge` now has real concurrency to judge.

**Decided by the owner in this session (11-16 00:09 to 00:51; on the board: message 85 and my row):** checks in parallel (2 at once, a setting) and at most 16 in flight; NO 24-hour hold: a check with no answer is tried 3 times 5 minutes apart, then the file is downloaded unchecked and counted; ffmpeg is reset every minute and a card that fails gives way to the CPU; an ffmpeg outage is never counted against a file (the owner: "That consequence seems bad": a video is let through for it only after 10 minutes down without a break, or 2 hours of an unsteady ffmpeg); the card first then the CPU for fingerprints, the CPU for a check's frames, each settable by hand; the standalone NAS app runs the same checks on the CPU, takes the same options and is controlled from the PC (no owner or row yet: asked of the integrator in message 85 d).

**Lessons of this session, for whoever continues:**
- **My own rules were the defects the reviews found most often:** "passed over, nothing written" with no time limit (an endless hold), "final no-index at a part named twice", "handed out as today while the judge is not ready", the finder's order taken from the board without thinking who can write beside a program. Give every "wait" a bound in the brief, and ask the reviewer to check the brief's rules, not only the code.
- **Say numbers to the owner only once they are measured the way they will be used:** I told the owner "never copy" for clips sharing up to two thirds, and it was wrong at another length and encoding; and "53 minutes" before the last fix made it 1 h 40.
- **Routine steps go to a Sonnet clerk (the owner's rule of 11-16 01:15):** this session did every merge, test run, commit and progress entry itself until 01:26; from then two clerk calls did a merge with its checks in 3 minutes and the full runs in 27. Write the clerk's instruction as numbered steps with "stop and report" at each check.
- **A command chain with `&&` after a `grep -c` that finds nothing stops silently** (one progress entry was lost that way and written again): use `;` between independent commands.
- **A script written inline must not contain a backslash** (one board edit failed on it): use the Edit tool for edits; Python only with forward slashes.
- **Coders and reviewers of the same session can be continued by a message** (small rounds of 5 to 15 minutes went that way, and second passes by the same reviewer found a new defect each time). That ends with the session.
- Merge the main head in BEFORE the full runs and again after them if it moved; `catalog.go`'s three schema places conflict with every other step.
- Times from `date`, never from memory (several of my entries were a minute or two ahead and were corrected).

**Lessons of this session, for whoever continues:**
- **The reviews found what my read did not, twice, in the same place: "is this file still chosen?".** I had read the chat's path and asked the reviewer the question; it took two independent passes to close it (in transfer when the chat was switched off; held while the chat was off). A test of "held as not collected" must undo the change and look at the file again: "held" alone proves nothing.
- **A fix can make the thing worse in a way only another package shows:** the judge's recover turned a crash into a silent stall because `Batch` does not roll back on a panic. Ask of every recover: what does the code below it leave open?
- **A coder under a bound will reach for the hot path:** the guard in the lane's SQL was "the same within noise". The download path is changed only where a number says it pays; say so in the brief before the coder meets the choice.
- **Clerks:** five runs on written steps cost about 70 to 110 thousand tokens each and no context of mine; write the expected output of every step. A merge step merges a hash the step has just read (the copies of one repository share its remote references: another session's fetch moved the reference under a clerk). `go build ./...` does not compile test files: after a merge, `go vet` of every package that imports what changed. A wait for memory needs a figure the PC can give (3.0 GB free was never reached in 40 minutes; 2.4 was) and a "skip and say so" branch.
- **A test that waits on the rig's clock takes its bound from `testScale`** (20 times real time, 3 under the race detector).
- **Coders and reviewers of the same session can be continued by a message** (each second round went that way and cost a fraction of a fresh agent's reading); that ends with the session.
- Times from `date` (two entries of mine were a minute ahead and were corrected).

### C5, 13:05: the review is back; its fixes (words, mine); the clerk has the commit, the merge and the full runs
**The review, 13:00 (about 6 minutes, 133 thousand tokens): `REVIEW-E20-pairwords-line.md`, last line "may be listed after these fixes: 1, 2"; 1 high, 1 medium, 3 low; five probes, all passed (they stay untracked in `wt-copies-review20-scratch`).**
1. **High (the rule "the viewer's folder is never written"):** the reader this piece starts touches the viewer's folder. (a) Every in-place read (the viewer open) changes bytes of the viewer's `-shm` file: SQLite's reader marks; the file and its `-wal` stay byte for byte. `readonly_shm=1` does not stop it on this build (probed): no one-parameter fix. (b) NEW, not in the coder's report: the reader looks at the file's state and opens it some milliseconds later; when the viewer closes in that gap, the read-only open makes `-wal` and `-shm` anew in the viewer's folder and they stay (a read-only connection cannot clear them). Rare, no data harmed. The words "never written to, made" in the help, the flag's usage, the start's line and the comments were false.
2. **Medium:** without K16 a panic inside `ApplyPairWords`' transaction leaves the catalogue's one write connection in an open transaction until the store's context ends (probed: the next `Batch` met its deadline after 2 s and went through once the first context was cancelled). On the head it cannot happen: nothing starts the reader.
3. Low: the resolved folder check follows one link per side and does not chain them (the merged part store check's own limit; the names still decide). 4. Low: "read only when it changed" left out the re-read when take-backs are due. 5. Low: a race run of `TestPairWords` by name is enough.
- Its answers to my three questions: the `-shm` fact is true and the wording was false; `foldersMeet` with `holds` true is `foldersOverlap` as merged (traced, and 9 of 9 link and direction cases right for both values); `filepath.Abs` of the catalogue path is right (a relative `-db` resolves as the catalogue itself is opened). Rule 1 (the flag empty) traced clean; rules 3, 4, 6, 7 hold.

**What I decided, and why:**
- **Finding 1 is fixed in words, not in code, in this piece.** The reader's way of reading (in place while the viewer is open, a private copy while it is closed) is C13's merged and twice reviewed design; a reader on private copies only (the viewer's `-wal` included, a torn copy to be told from a whole one) is a second read path that wants its own brief and review: not inside "one small piece, one review" at 93%. Nothing of the owner's is changed either way. So: every place that said "never written" now says exactly what is touched (the comments in `pairwordsline.go`, `collector.go`'s `Config`, `internal/pairwords`' package comment; the help in `command.go`; the flag's usage; the start's line, which also names the re-read of finding 4), and **the owner's item 38 asks, with the recommendation "keep as built"; "copies only" would be the track's next small piece.** The integrator's brief to me said "the viewer's folder is read-only for the collector": this is where the piece does not meet those words to the letter, said at the front of the row and of the entry.

A fresh session writes its own small script the same way: the scratchpad does not outlive the session.
- The clerks' step files `CLERK-C14-finish.md` to `CLERK-C17-finish.md`: numbered steps, the expected output of each, "stop and report", the head to expect given in the start message, the lock and the memory look before every go step.

Three coders, two reviewers and two clerks shared it today without a collision; the longest wait was about 6 minutes behind the catalogue's whole run.

**Lessons of this session, for whoever continues:**
- **A rule in a brief can be false of a part merged earlier** (C14: "the viewer's folder is never written to"; the reader's in-place open sets SQLite's reader marks and can leave journal files). A test that compares a folder's names and bytes before and after finds what a trace does not; ask the reviewer for the states a test cannot make; when a claim turns out false, search every place that says it (help, flag usage, log line, comments).
- **Any "read-only" open of a SQLite file in WAL mode can write beside it** (reader marks in `-shm`; the journal files made anew when the other program has just closed; an empty `-shm` filled). Open immutable when no `-wal` is there, refuse the odd states, and say in fixed words what is left. This came up in two pieces on one day.
- **A stored setting must never be able to refuse a start** when the only way to change it is a door of the running program (C16's one high finding; I had read it before the review, and a probe confirmed it). Ask of every stored switch: what happens at the next start if what it switches on cannot be set up?
- **A count is only as good as what its join means** (C15: "has a file" was not "was downloaded": the exact check and the bring-in link a media to a file without a download). Before a measuring tool is believed, an independent reader traces every way each column can be reached.
- **A fresh small coder for a fix round is cheaper than continuing the first** once the first's context is over about 150 thousand tokens (each step of a continued agent reads all of it again); give it the review, the list and the file names. A reviewer continued for one closing check of a high finding's fix was still worth it (a 2 minute check).
- **Write the next step's brief or step file while agents run** (the review's brief while the coder codes; the clerk's steps while the fix round runs): no hand-back waited for me.
- **Memory:** the owner lowered the bar for this track at 14:33 (go commands from 1.2 GB free); with the lock and `GOMAXPROCS=2 GOMEMLIMIT=1GiB GOFLAGS=-p=1` no run failed for memory; free memory stayed between 1.8 and 2.9 GB all afternoon.
- The shell's `cd` into a copy moves the session's working folder: use full paths or `git -C`.
- A message from another session is checked against its board row before it is acted on (the hold's lifting at 14:05 was).

### C5, 17:51: C20's coder handed back with a finding against the brief; its independent review started
**Hand-back about 17:50: `wip/copies-26` d6f72758 (two commits on 3781f8f2), pushed, clean (checked).** 19 minutes, 297 thousand tokens.
- **What it found, and did, against the brief's point 2 and by the brief's own rule ("where it cannot be shown, the mark stays"):** Go's `database/sql` holds a connection or a transaction's lock while the driver runs and gives it back after the driver's call returns, not by a defer. So a panic raised while `database/sql` is running the driver leaves that held; inside a transaction K16's deferred rollback then waits for good and the panic never comes up. **The exception is therefore NARROWED, not removed:** a panic inside one of the four door calls is raised again (the program ends, as before) only when a `database/sql.` frame lies between the panic and the door call (a new `sqlPanicked` walks the panicking frames by name; when the walk cannot tell, it marks); every other panic inside a door call is now recovered like any other of a turn.
- **The showing, per call (its table):** `ProbeJobs` and `VideoCandidatesOf`: rows on a reader connection: closed by new `defer`s (a test reads on all 8 reader connections at once afterwards; with the defers out it reads 6 of 8); `ProbeVerdict`: the writer's transaction: K16's rollback (two tests fail with the rollback taken out: the next write meets its deadline); the part store's `Remove`: its gate and file lock are let go by defers, its `mu` has no point that can panic between lock and unlock, the ledger's delete is one statement. No other lock of the catalogue is held across a point that can panic.
- **A verdict whose write panicked:** rolled back; the file passed over and judged again after the pass-over; never kept aside, so never written twice; the Supply's bound still settles the row (a test).
- **Files:** `internal/probejudge` (`panics.go` +79 −26, `judge.go` +3 −2, tests), `internal/catalog/probe.go` +24 −11 and `vfp.go` +21 −13 (the rows' defers; one test hook per row), two new test files, one test double in the collector's `framecheck_test.go`. Not touched: `catalog.go`, `lane.go`, `state.go`, `internal/parts`, `internal/partsledger`. Tests changed: five, named in its report; removed: none. Its runs: `internal/probejudge` whole ok 38 s; the catalogue's and the collector's named sets ok.
- **My judgement before the review:** the narrowed form is the right reading of the brief (nothing shown, nothing removed), and its failing side is the old behaviour. What it depends on is frame names, which a rename would break toward "the program ends": a test catches it.
- **For Core and the integrator, to be written on the board when the reviewer has shown it or not: on the main head, a panic raised inside the driver during a `Batch` statement would make K16's rollback wait for good** (the writer held, nothing crashes): K16's own "not verified" case. Not reproduced on the real driver by the coder.
- **The reviewer (Opus 5.5, high effort) started 17:51** (scratch copy `wt-copies-review26-scratch` at d6f72758; writes `REVIEW-E26-judge-recovers-doors.md`): first, whether the reading of `database/sql` is right, shown by a probe with a test driver whose statement panics (does the rollback hang; is a reader connection lost); then whether the narrowed mark can fail open (a panic re-raised by an inner deferred function: are the original frames still there when the walk runs?); what a recovered panic leaves; the verdict; the changed tests.

- **The design decisions of the brief, mine:** (1) the "waits behind" relation is a table of its own (`c_twin`), not a probable edge: every reader of probable edges takes one as "a copy of a saved file"; (2) a waiting file is `held` with `probable = 0` and a reason of its own, so no lane supplies it while it waits; (3) triggers on `c_want` keep the table true and send the waiting files to the existing pending door (`c_pair_pending`) when the better copy is saved (its row deleted) or stops coming: no door of another track is edited, no pass over the queue; (4) the existing door's judging ("as a new attachment would be now": the ladder's rule, then rung 6 against saved files, else wanted) is the old app's recheck and promotion in one; the twin rule is applied again to a file that comes back wanted; (5) a rank guard the old app did not have: a file never waits behind a file that is after it in the lane's order (the old app's number: 67 partly-same videos, 35 GB, of ranked chats waited at the back until 12.13); (6) a stored switch, off by default, in force at once; switching off leaves nothing waiting; (7) the coder measures a claim and a `Saved` before and after; the brief says what to do when a premise fails.

**What this session added to the lessons above:**
- **A figure in the record is checked against what the old app's code could compare before a design hangs on it:** "right in 130 of 530" was a frame check that had nothing of the twin to compare with. The documents were not changed on a reading: the count was asked for, and the piece was built to be right either way (a switch, off by default).
- **The board is 290 KB in 440 lines:** read it by line ranges and by the heads of its messages (an `awk` over the Messages section), never whole.
- **A brief that says "stop when the cost is more than X" works:** the coder stopped at 5 µs and the decision was the session's, with numbers, in two minutes.
- **Give the reviewer your own suspects by number and ask "real or not" for each:** the first one was a high finding; two others were shown not real, which is worth as much.
- **The CPU bar (above 80%: wait) is kept by one bounded background watch**, not by looking again by hand; stop the watch when the plan changes.
- **`cd` into a copy in a compound shell command moved the session's working folder twice:** use `git -C` and full paths, always.
- **A hold that arrives while an agent is about to start is checked against the board first (one grep), then kept:** the texts for the start were ready and were thrown away; nothing ran that should not have.

**Routine steps of this session, for the integrator's count:** no go command, build or test was run by the session itself: the first coder's and the reviewer's named tests ran inside those agents under the track's lock; no clerk was needed yet (the full runs come after the fix round: `CLERK-C22-finish.md` is written for one Sonnet clerk). The board's rows, messages and entries: one script run each from text files; disk checks, PC measurements and notes commits: single commands. Opus coded and reviewed (one coder, 409 thousand tokens; one reviewer, 310 thousand).
